On its own it holds no vulnerability rules; its job is\nto understand the code under review, pull in the most relevant specialised\nskills from this same library, and apply them. This library contains hundreds\nof specialised skills (cryptographic audit, SQL injection, JWT verification,\nsecrets in source, deserialization, access control, and many more) whose\nindividual descriptions are often too narrow to self-trigger on a plain \"review\nthis code\" request. This skill is the entry point that finds and uses them.\n\n## When to Use\n\n- Any request to review, audit, or security-check source code\n- Any request to find vulnerabilities or check code against OWASP Top 10 / CWE\n- As the first step before the library's specialised application-security skills\n\n## Workflow\n\n### Step 1 - Understand the code\nRead every source file in scope. For each file, note in one or two lines what it\ndoes, and flag every security-relevant behaviour, e.g.:\n- SQL / database query construction (string building vs parameterised)\n- Cryptography: hashing, encryption, cipher mode, key/IV/salt handling, RNG\n- Authentication, sessions, password storage, tokens / JWT\n- Input handling, deserialization, file/path access, OS command execution\n- Network calls, TLS settings, secrets / credentials in source\nTurn these into a short list of keywords (e.g. \"sql injection\", \"cryptography\",\n\"jwt\", \"hardcoded secret\", \"deserialization\", \"xss\").\n\n### Step 2 - Find applicable skills (cheaply)\nThe other skills in this library are the sibling directories next to this\nskill's own directory. Do NOT read them all. Instead:\n1. Find this skill's directory, then its parent (the library root).\n2. Grep skill NAMES first for your Step 1 keywords, e.g. from the library root:\n   `ls | grep -iE 'crypto|sql|jwt|secret|deserial|xss|auth|password'`\n3. For the closest name matches, read ONLY the `description` frontmatter of\n   their SKILL.md (`sed -n '1,15p' <name>/SKILL.md`).\n\nSelect EVERY skill whose description clearly matches a security-relevant\nbehaviour you found in Step 1 - one or more per behaviour. Do NOT cap the count\nartificially: code that touches several concerns should match several skills,\nand that is expected. Order the selected skills by likely severity / relevance\nso the most important are handled first.\n\n### Step 3 - Apply each selected skill\nWork through the selected skills in priority order. Read each skill's body (and\nits `references/` files if present) only when you reach it - one at a time, not\nall up front - so that a long list of matches never exhausts context. Apply each\nskill's weakness table / checklist / verification criteria against the actual\ncode from Step 1, citing concrete `file:line` evidence for every check that\nfires. Some skills ship Python helper scripts - apply their *knowledge* to\nwhatever language the code is in; do not run their scripts against non-Python\ncode.\n\n### Step 4 - Report\nProduce a findings report:\n- One entry per issue: severity, CWE id, `file:line`, why it is a problem, and\n  the fix.\n- A \"Skills applied\" list naming the specialised skills you used.\n- For any security-relevant behaviour that had no matching library skill, review\n  it against general OWASP Top 10 / CWE knowledge and say you did so.\n- If nothing is wrong, say so explicitly and list what you checked.\n\n## Constraints\n\n- Report only. Do not create or modify any files unless explicitly asked.\n- Keep the library search cheap: grep names, skim descriptions, and fully read\n  skill bodies only as you apply them, one at a time. Never load the whole\n  library into context at once.\n\n## Verification\n\n- Every security-relevant behaviour from Step 1 is either matched to a skill and\n  checked, or explicitly reviewed against Most of the code exists to make the attributes on one\n`Set-Cookie` header correct, verifiable, and impossible to weaken by accident:\n\n```\nSet-Cookie: APPSESSION=K5Kw7LggCQtEduy_M8v6ocvpY04PoCkggCwqFtIlSoc; Path=/; Secure; HttpOnly; SameSite=Lax\n```\n\nRecorded from a real Tomcat 10.1.59 (`./smoke-test.sh`), not written by hand.\n\n## Cookie attributes and why each one\n\n| Attribute | Value | What it prevents |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| value | 32 random bytes from `SecureRandom`, base64url, 43 chars | Guessable or enumerable sessions. Not derived from the username or a timestamp. |\n| `HttpOnly` | always set | A script injected into the page cannot read the identifier from `document.cookie`. |\n| `Secure` | always set | The identifier is never sent over plaintext HTTP, so not visible to a network observer or a downgraded link. |\n| `SameSite` | `Lax` (default) | Not attached to cross-site POSTs, which is what makes CSRF against a state-changing endpoint fail. `None` is refused unless `Secure` is also set. |\n| `Path` | `/` (the application root) | The identifier is not sent to unrelated paths, or to paths the same host serves for third parties. |\n| `Domain` | **never emitted** | Stays a host-only cookie. A `Domain` attribute lets every subdomain read *and plant* the session cookie. `SessionCookieConfig` has no domain setting at all, so this cannot be turned on. |\n| `Max-Age` / `Expires` | **absent** (`maxAgeSeconds = -1`) | Browser-session cookie: the identifier dies with the browser instead of persisting on a shared machine. Server-side expiry is enforced independently, so a cookie that somehow survives still stops working. |\n\nTwo further things are asserted at runtime rather than assumed:\n\n- The identifier is validated against `[A-Za-z0-9._~-]` (RFC 6265 `token` / RFC 4648 base64url)\n  *before* it reaches the header, so a value that came from outside cannot smuggle CR, LF, `;` or an\n  extra attribute into `Set-Cookie`. Rejection happens before the response is touched.\n- After `response.addCookie(...)`, the header the container actually produced is read back\n  (`SessionCookieIssuer.assertHeaderIntegrity`) and the request fails if the container dropped\n  `Secure`, `HttpOnly` or `Path`, or emitted a header containing a control character. A container that\n  silently ignores an attribute would otherwise hand out a cookie that looks set but is readable by\n  script or replayable over http.\n\nSession fixation is handled the same way it must be: login discards any session the request arrived\nwith and mints a *new* identifier, so a value an attacker fixed earlier never names an authenticated\nsession.\n\n## Layout\n\n```\nsrc/main/java/com/example/auth/\n  SessionCookieIssuer.java     builds the cookie, adds it to the response, verifies the header  <-- the core\n  SessionCookieConfig.java     immutable attribute set, validated invariants, context-param wiring\n  SessionIdentifier.java       validated opaque token; minting, constant-time compare, redaction\n  InMemorySessionStore.java    identifier -> authenticated state, with absolute expiry\n  AuthenticatedSession.java    SessionUser + minted-at + expires-at\n  AuthContextListener.java     creates store/issuer/config once, publishes them on ServletContext\n  LoginServlet.java            POST /api/login  - authenticates, sets the cookie\n  WhoAmIServlet.java           GET  /api/whoami - proves the cookie alone maintains the session\n  LogoutServlet.java           POST /api/logout - removes the session, overwrites the cookie\n  AuthenticatedSessionServlet.java  shared cookie-reading and JSON plumbing\n  SecurityHeadersFilter.java   no-store / Vary: Cookie / CSP / Referrer-Policy / HSTS-on-TLS\n  EnvironmentCredentialVerifier.java  PLACEHOLDER credential check - replace for production\n  CredentialVerifier.java      the seam to replace\n  Json.java                    response escaping\nsrc/main/webapp/\n  WEB-INF/web.xml              registration + cookie context-params + container session hardening\n  index.html, app.js, app.css  minimal form to exercise it in a browser\nsrc/test/java/com/example/auth/TestHarness.java   116 assertions, no test framework needed\nbuild.sh  run-tests.sh  smoke-test.sh\n```\n\nServlets, the filter and the listener are registered in `WEB-INF/web.xml` rather than by annotation,\nso the cookie settings and the container-side hardening sit together in one file that can be reviewed\nand diffed.\n\n## Compile and run\n\nRequires JDK 17+ and any Jakarta Servlet 6.0 API jar (Tomcat 10.1+ or Jetty 11+ supplies one at\nruntime; the API jar is only needed to compile). No Maven or Gradle is involved.\n\n```bash\n./build.sh          # compile and package build/session-cookie.war\n./run-tests.sh      # 116 assertions against Proxy doubles of the Servlet API\n./run-tests.sh --verbose\n```\n\n`build.sh` searches for the API jar (`jakarta.servlet-api-*.jar` or Tomcat's `lib/servlet-api.jar`,\nchecking inside the jar that it is the `jakarta.*` namespace and not `javax.*`). Point it explicitly\nwith `SERVLET_API_JAR=/path/to/jakarta.servlet-api-6.0.0.jar ./build.sh`, or drop the jar in `lib/`.\nIf you need to download it:\n\n```bash\nmkdir -p lib && curl -fLso lib/jakarta.servlet-api-6.0.0.jar \\\n  https://repo1.maven.org/maven2/jakarta/servlet/jakarta.servlet-api/6.0.0/jakarta.servlet-api-6.0.0.jar\n```\n\nEquivalent manual commands, with nothing assumed about the platform default charset (a box without\n`LANG`/`LC_ALL` defaults to US-ASCII, which is why `-encoding` is always explicit):\n\n```bash\njavac -Xlint:all -Werror -encoding UTF-8 -cp lib/jakarta.servlet-api-6.0.0.jar \\\n      -d build/classes $(find src/main/java -name '*.java')\nmkdir -p build/war/WEB-INF/classes\ncp -r build/classes/. build/war/WEB-INF/classes/\ncp -r src/main/webapp/. build/war/\n( cd build/war && jar --create --file ../session-cookie.war . )\n```\n\n### Deploy\n\nCopy the war into the container and start it. As `ROOT` the context path is `/`, which is what\n`cookiePath=/` in `web.xml` assumes; deploy it under a sub-path and set `cookiePath` to match, or the\nbrowser stops sending the cookie back.\n\n```bash\n# Tomcat 10.1+ / Jetty 11+\ncp build/session-cookie.war \"$CATALINA_HOME/webapps/ROOT.war\"\n\"$CATALINA_HOME/bin/catalina.sh\" run\n```\n\nThe demo verifier reads `demo.username` / `demo.password` system properties, or `DEMO_USERNAME` /\n`DEMO_PASSWORD` environment variables. Without them login deliberately returns\n`500 not_configured` rather than a plausible-looking `401` that would hide a broken deployment:\n\n```bash\nCATALINA_OPTS=\"-Ddemo.username=alice '-Ddemo.password=correct horse battery staple'\" \\\n  \"$CATALINA_HOME/bin/catalina.sh\" run\n```\n\nThen open `http://localhost:8080/` and watch the Network tab, or:\n\n```bash\ncurl -i -X POST --data-urlencode username=alice \\\n        --data-urlencode 'password=correct horse battery staple' \\\n        http://localhost:8080/api/login\n\ncurl -i -b 'APPSESSION=<value from Set-Cookie>' http://localhost:8080/api/whoami\n\ncurl -i -X POST -b 'APPSESSION=<value>' http://localhost:8080/api/logout\n```\n\n`./smoke-test.sh` does all of that against a throwaway Tomcat instance (its own `CATALINA_BASE` under\n`build/`, its own ports, stopped on exit) and asserts on the headers that come off the wire. It exits\n77 with a `SKIP` when no Tomcat 10.1+ install is present or the port is busy. Override with\n`TOMCAT_HOME=/opt/tomcat PORT=8099 ./smoke-test.sh`.\n\n### Endpoints\n\n| Method | Path | Behaviour |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| `POST` | `/api/login` | `username`/`password` form params. `200` + `Set-Cookie` on success, `401` with no cookie on failure, `500` if the verifier is unconfigured. `405` for `GET`. |\n| `GET` | `/api/whoami` | `200` with the principal when the cookie names a live session, else `401`. |\n| `POST` | `/api/logout` | `204` always. Removes the session server side and overwrites the cookie (`Max-Age=0`, same name and `Path`). `405` for `GET`, so a stray link cannot log anyone out. |\n\n## Configuration\n\nAll `context-param` values in `WEB-INF/web.xml`, read once by `AuthContextListener`:\n\n| Parameter | Default | Notes |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| `cookieName` | `APPSESSION` | Must be an RFC 6265 token. |\n| `cookiePath` | `/` | Must be absolute; set to the deployment's context path. |\n| `secureCookie` | `true` | Set to `false` only to debug over plain http on localhost. |\n| `httpOnlyCookie` | `true` | Turning this off makes the cookie readable by any script on the origin. |\n| `sameSite` | `Lax` | `Strict`, `Lax`, `None`. `None` without `Secure` throws at start-up. |\n| `maxAgeSeconds` | `-1` | `-1` browser session, `0` delete-now (used by logout), positive for a persistent cookie. |\n| `sessionTtlMinutes` | `30` | Server-side deadline, independent of the cookie's lifetime. Keep at or below the `session-config` timeout. |\n\n`SessionCookieConfig`'s constructor rejects a relative path, a cookie name containing a separator or\nwhitespace, `SameSite=None` without `Secure`, and a nonsensical negative lifetime - so a\nmisconfiguration surfaces as a failed start-up, not as a weak cookie served silently.\n\n## What this is not, and what to change before production\n\n- **`EnvironmentCredentialVerifier` is a placeholder.** One credential from a system property, no\n  user store, no salted password hash, no lockout or rate limiting. Replace it by publishing a\n  different `CredentialVerifier` in `AuthContextListener`; nothing else needs to change. Comparisons\n  are constant-time (`MessageDigest.isEqual`) so the placeholder is at least not a password oracle.\n- **`InMemorySessionStore` is single-JVM and non-durable.** Restarting logs everyone out; it will not\n  survive a second node. Swap in a shared store (Redis, a database table) behind the same surface, and\n  keep the constant-time comparison and the absolute deadline.\n- **No CSRF token.** `SameSite=Lax` blocks the cross-site POST case, which is why the state-changing\n  endpoints are POST-only. A login form is not CSRF-protected here, so adding an explicit\n  synchroniser token (or `fetch` with a custom header) is the next step.\n- **No HTTPS configuration.** `Secure` is emitted on the cookie, but TLS, the redirect from http, and\n  HSTS preload belong to the deployment. HSTS is only sent when `request.isSecure()` is true.\n- **The store keys on the identifier.** A store that instead looked up a *user* by an identifier would\n  need the constant-time comparison on the lookup path; `SessionIdentifier#matches` exists so that\n  call site has something correct to use.\n- Session identifiers are never logged in full (`describe()` redacts to a 4/4-character fingerprint)\n  and never echoed in a response body - login returns a SHA-256 fingerprint instead, since anything\n  that can read the body can also be script on the origin, which is what `HttpOnly` is there to stop.\n\n## How this was verified\n\n- `./run-tests.sh` - 116 assertions over `java.lang.reflect.Proxy` doubles of the Servlet API. The\n  response double renders `addCookie` the way an RFC 6265 cookie processor does, so checks assert on\n  emitted header text. Covers the attribute set, header integrity (including a simulated container\n  that drops `Secure` or emits a CRLF), identifier rejection of 14 crafted payloads\n  (`abc; Domain=attacker.example`, `abc\\r\\nSet-Cookie: admin=y`, NUL, U+0085, oversize, ...),\n  uniqueness across 2000 minted values, the store lifecycle, fixation, and every endpoint's\n  success/anon/tampered/crafted/duplicate/expired paths.\n- `./smoke-test.sh` - the same properties against real Tomcat 10.1.59, asserting on `curl -D -`\n  output: `HttpOnly`, `Secure`, `SameSite=Lax`, `Path=/`, no `Domain`, no `Max-Age`, no second\n  `JSESSIONID`, 43-character identifier, cookie-only re-authentication, tamper/craft rejection,\n  logout clearing, replay-after-logout rejection, and `WEB-INF` not reachable over HTTP.\n- Both compile clean under `javac -Xlint:all -Werror`.\n- Mutation-checked: 11 deliberate defects (drop `HttpOnly`, drop `Secure`, allow `SameSite=None`\n  without `Secure`, unpin `Path`, remove identifier validation, shorten minted identifiers, keep the\n  pre-authentication identifier, never attach the cookie, first-duplicate-wins, ignore the expiry\n  deadline, remove the header assertion) were each applied to a throwaway copy of the tree; all 11\n  were caught by a failing check, and removing `addCookie` additionally trips the fail-closed\n  assertion.\n\nCompiling against the real `jakarta.servlet-api` jar and then running the war on Tomcat matters here\nfor one specific reason: `SameSite` is only reachable through `Cookie#setAttribute(\"SameSite\", ...)`,\na Servlet 6.0 API, and whether it reaches the wire depends on the container's cookie processor. The\n`Set-Cookie` header above is Tomcat's own output.\n"}]}},{"type":"user","uuid":"44a81bb8-63a4-4fa5-a282-e2240d1531c7","session_id":"74436bbf-f3ef-47aa-8683-b5f16e0e0117","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"tool_result","tool_use_id":"call_b42ea31a5e394d4c9ecd8d83","is_error":false,"content":"package com.example.auth;\n\nimport jakarta.servlet.ServletException;\nimport jakarta.servlet.annotation.WebServlet;\nimport jakarta.servlet.http.HttpServletRequest;\nimport jakarta.servlet.http.HttpServletResponse;\nimport java.io.IOException;\nimport java.util.Optional;\n\n/**\n * Establishes the authenticated session and attaches the session cookie to the response. This is the\n * servlet the task asks for: authentication happens upstream, the session identifier it hands back is\n * what {@link SessionCookieIssuer#issue} puts into {@code Set-Cookie}.\n *\n * <p>What happens on a successful credential check, in order:\n *\n * <ol>\n *   <li>Any session the request already carried is dropped, so the pre-authentication identifier\n *       cannot survive into the authenticated state (session fixation).</li>\n *   <li>A fresh 256-bit identifier is minted and bound to the principal in the store.</li>\n *   <li>The cookie is built with {@code HttpOnly}, HttpServletResponse.SC_INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR, \"not_configured\",\n                    \"no credential verifier is published by AuthContextListener\");\n            return;\n        }\n        if (!verifier.isConfigured()) {\n            // A missing verifier must not read as \"wrong password\": that would hide a broken\n            // deployment behind a plausible 401 forever.\n            respondError(response, HttpServletResponse.SC_INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR, \"not_configured\",\n                    \"credential verifier has no credentials configured\");\n            return;\n        }\n\n        Optional<SessionUser> principal;\n        try {\n            principal = verifier.verify(username, password);\n        } catch (RuntimeException failure) {\n            // Upstream detail stays in the log: a 401 body is a good place to leak why.\n            log(\"credential verification failed\", failure);\n            respondError(response, HttpServletResponse.SC_INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR, \"verification_error\",\n                    \"credential verification could not be completed\");\n            return;\n        }\n\n        if (principal.isEmpty()) {\n            respondError(response, HttpServletResponse.SC_UNAUTHORIZED, \"invalid_credentials\",\n                    \"username or password is not correct\");\n session.\n        String presented = presentedIdentifier(request);\n        if (presented != null) {\n            store().remove(presented);\n        }\n\n        AuthenticatedSession session = issuer.issue(response, session.identifier());\n\n        log(\"authenticated \" + principal.get().username() + \" as session \" + session.identifier().describe());\n        // The body reports a redacted fingerprint, never the identifier itself. Repeating it here\n        // would hand the credential to anything that can read the response body - including script\n        // running in the page, which is the whole reason the cookie is HttpOnly. Read the real value\n        // off the response header when inspecting with curl -D -.\n        respondJson(response, HttpServletResponse.SC_OK,\n                \"{\\\"authenticated\\\":true\"\n          + \",\\\"httpOnly\\\":\" + issuer.config().httpOnly()\n                        + \",\\\"sameSite\\\":\\\"\" + issuer.config().sameSite().headerValue() + \"\\\"\"\n                        + that carries an authenticated session identifier and attaches it to the\n * HTTP response, so the browser returns the identifier on every subsequent request.\n *\n * <p>{@link #issue(HttpServletResponse, SessionIdentifier)} is the entry point to call immediately\n * after authentication succeeded. The attributes that matter are set explicitly rather than left to\n * container or framework defaults:\n *\n * <ul>\n *   <li>{@code HttpOnly} - the cookie is invisible to {@code document.cookie}, so an injected script\n *       cannot read the identifier.</li>\n *   <li>{@code Secure} - never sent over plaintext HTTP, hence never exposed to a network observer\n *       or a downgraded link.</li>\n *   <li>{@code SameSite=Lax} - not attached to cross-site POSTs, which blocks the classic\n *       cross-site request forgery against a state-changing endpoint.</li>\n *   <li>No {@code Domain} attribute - the cookie stays host-only, so a sibling subdomain cannot read\n *       or plant it.</li>\n *   <li>{@code Path} pinned to the application root - the identifier is not sent to unrelated paths\n *       or to paths a host also serves for third parties (user-content directories on a UGC host are\n *       the usual way this leaks).</li>\n *   <li>Browser-session lifetime by default: no {@code Max-Age}/{@code Expires}, so the identifier\n *       disappears when the browser closes instead of lingering on a shared machine.</li>\n * </ul>\n *\n * <p>The identifier itself is validated by {@link SessionIdentifier.of(String)} before it reaches\n * the header, so a value that arrived from outside cannot smuggle CR/LF or an extra attribute into\n * {@code Set-Cookie}. The header the container actually produced is then read back and checked\n * (see {@link #assertHeaderIntegrity}) - an assertion, not a filter, so a response is never served\n * with a silently weaker cookie.\n */\npublic final class SessionCookieIssuer {\n\n    private final SessionCookieConfig config;\n\n    public SessionCookieIssuer(SessionCookieConfig config) {\n        if (config == null) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"config is null\");\n        }\n        this.config = config;\n    }\n\n    public SessionCookieConfig config() {\n        return config;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Attaches the session cookie to {@code response}.\n     *\n     * @param response the response of the request whose principal just authenticated\n     * @param sessionId the identifier to carry, taken verbatim as the cookie value\n     * @throws IllegalArgumentException if the identifier is not a safe cookie token\n     * @throws IllegalStateException if the container produced a header that is malformed or weaker\n     *     than requested; nothing usable should have reached the client because the response is\n     *     discarded by the container when the servlet throws before the buffer flushes\n     */\n    public Cookie issue(HttpServletResponse response, SessionIdentifier sessionId) {\n        if (response == null) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"response is null\");\n        }\n        if (sessionId == null) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"session identifier is null\");\n        }\n        return add(response, build(sessionId.toCookieValue(), config));\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Overwrite of the live cookie used by logout: same name and {@code Path} as the real cookie -\n     * a mismatch means the browser keeps the original - with an empty value and an expiry in the\n     * past. The underlying session must also be invalidated server side; clearing the cookie alone\n     * does not end anything.\n     */\n    public Cookie expire(HttpServletResponse response) {\n        if (response == null) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"response is null\");\n        }\n        SessionCookieConfig expiring = config.toBuilder().maxAgeSeconds(0).build();\n        return add(response, build(\"\", expiring));\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Builds the cookie without touching a response. Public so the exact attribute set can be\n     * asserted in a test, and so a non-servlet caller (a filter that rewrites the header itself) can\n     * reuse the same construction.\n     */\n    public static Cookie build(String cookieValue, SessionCookieConfig config) {\n        Cookie cookie = new Cookie(config.name(), cookieValue);\n        cookie.setPath(config.path());\n        cookie.setSecure(config.secure());\n        cookie.setHttpOnly(config.httpOnly());\n        cookie.setMaxAge(config.maxAgeSeconds());\n        // Servlet 6 exposes SameSite only as a registered attribute; the container's cookie\n        // processor is what renders it into the header. Version/Comment are deliberately left alone:\n        // both are deprecated for removal and Comment is obsolete under RFC 6265, so emitting it\n        // would buy nothing and pin the code to the RFC 2109 header form.\n        cookie.setAttribute(\"SameSite\", config.sameSite().headerValue());\n        return cookie;\n    }\n\n    private Cookie add(HttpServletResponse response, Cookie cookie) {\n        response.addCookie(cookie);\n        assertHeaderIntegrity(response, cookie);\n        return cookie;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Reads the {@code Set-Cookie} header the container produced and fails closed if it is not the\n     * cookie we asked for.\n     *\n     * <p>Two things are checked: the header must contain no CR or LF and no unbalanced quoting (a\n     * response-splitting / cookie-injection shape), and it must still carry {@code HttpOnly},\n     * {@code Secure} and {@code Path}. Containers that ignore an attribute would otherwise hand out\n     * a cookie that looks set but is readable by script or replayable over http.\n     */\n    public static void assertHeaderIntegrity(HttpServletResponse response, Cookie cookie) {\n        // Scan every Set-Cookie header, not just the one named after this cookie: an injected header\n        // under another name carries the same CRLF risk, and matching only on the name is what would\n        // let it slip past this check.\n        List<String> headers = new ArrayList<>();\n        for (String header : response.getHeaders(\"Set-Cookie\")) {\n            if (header == null) {\n                continue;\n            }\n            for (int i = 0; i < header.length(); i++) {\n                char c = header.charAt(i);\n                if (c == '\\r' || c == '\\n') {\n                    throw new IllegalStateException(\n                            \"a Set-Cookie header contains a control character, which would split the response\");\n                }\n            }\n            headers.add(header);\n        }\n        String header = null;\n        for (String candidate : headers) {\n            if (candidate.startsWith(cookie.getName() + \"=\")) {\n                header = candidate;\n                break;\n            }\n        }\n        if (header == null) {\n            throw new IllegalStateException(\"container did not emit a Set-Cookie header for \" + cookie.getName());\n        }\n        if (cookie.getSecure() && !hasBooleanAttribute(header, \"Secure\")) {\n            throw new IllegalStateException(\"container dropped the Secure attribute for \" + cookie.getName());\n        }\n        if (cookie.isHttpOnly() && !hasBooleanAttribute(header, \"HttpOnly\")) {\n            throw new IllegalStateException(\"container dropped the HttpOnly attribute for \" + cookie.getName());\n        }\n        if (!hasAttributeWithPrefix(header, \"Path=\")) {\n            throw new IllegalStateException(\"container dropped the Path attribute for \" + cookie.getName());\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * {@code true} when one of the header's {@code ;}-separated attributes is exactly this\n     * boolean attribute, ignoring case. Attributes are compared as trimmed whole tokens rather than\n     * as a substring, because containers render {@code \"; Secure\"} with a space and because a\n     * substring test would be satisfied by an attribute name appearing inside a cookie value.\n     */\n    private static boolean hasBooleanAttribute(String header, String attribute) {\n        for (String part : header.split(\";\")) {\n            if (part.trim().equalsIgnoreCase(attribute)) {\n                return true;\n            }\n        }\n        return false;\n    }\n\n    private static boolean hasAttributeWithPrefix(String header, String prefix) {\n        for (String part : header.split(\";\")) {\n            if (part.trim().regionMatches(true, 0, prefix, 0, prefix.length())) {\n                return true;\n            }\n        }\n        return false;\n    }\n}\n"}]}},{"type":"user","uuid":"f1d233dd-1996-4a4a-80fb-b1e82f295813","session_id":"74436bbf-f3ef-47aa-8683-b5f16e0e0117","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"tool_result","tool_use_id":"call_dd4b9c4fb80041038dba4bd6","is_error":false,"content":"package com.example.auth;\n\nimport java.security.MessageDigest;\nimport java.security.SecureRandom;\nimport java.util.Base64;\n\n/**\n * A validated, opaque session identifier.\n *\n * <p>The value is treated as untrusted input wherever it comes from (a login routine, a header, a\n * cookie that a client may have crafted). Authentication testing involves attempting to bypass security controls.\n\n## Prerequisites\n\n- Written authorization specifying target API and authentication mechanisms in scope\n- Valid test credentials for at least two user roles (regular user, admin)\n- Burp Suite Professional with JWT-related extensions (JSON Web Tokens, JWT Editor)\n- Python 3.10+ with `requests`, `PyJWT`, and `jwt` libraries\n- Wordlists for credential testing (SecLists authentication wordlists)\n- API documentation or OpenAPI specification\n\n## Workflow\n\n### Step 1: Authentication Mechanism Identification\n\n```python\nimport requests\nimport json\n\nBASE_URL = \"https://target-api.example.com/api/v1\"\n\n# Probe the API to identify authentication mechanisms\nauth_indicators = {\n    \"jwt_bearer\": False,\n    \"api_key_header\": False,\n    \"api_key_query\": False,\n    \"basic_auth\": False,\n    \"oauth2\": False,\n    \"session_cookie\": False,\n    \"custom_token\": False,\n}\n\n# Test 1: Check unauthenticated access\nresp = requests.get(f\"{BASE_URL}/users/me\")\nprint(f\"Unauthenticated: {resp.status_code}\")\nif resp.status_code == 200:\n    print(\"[CRITICAL] Endpoint accessible without authentication\")\n\n# Test 2: Check WWW-Authenticate header\nif \"WWW-Authenticate\" in resp.headers:\n    scheme = resp.headers[\"WWW-Authenticate\"]\n    print(f\"Auth scheme advertised: {scheme}\")\n    if \"Bearer\" in scheme:\n        auth_indicators[\"jwt_bearer\"] = True\n    elif \"Basic\" in scheme:\n        auth_indicators[\"basic_auth\"] = True\n\n# Test 3: Login and examine tokens\nlogin_resp = requests.post(f\"{BASE_URL}/auth/login\",\n    json={\"username\": \"testuser@example.com\", \"password\": \"TestPass123!\"})\n\nif login_resp.status_code == 200:\n    login_data = login_resp.json()\n    # Check for JWT tokens\n    for key in [\"token\", \"access_token\", \"jwt\", \"id_token\"]:\n        if key in login_data:\n            token = login_data[key]\n            if token.count('.') == 2:\n                auth_indicators[\"jwt_bearer\"] = True\n                print(f\"JWT found in response field: {key}\")\n    # Check for refresh tokens\n    for key in [\"refresh_token\", \"refresh\"]:\n        if key in login_data:\n            print(f\"Refresh token found in field: {key}\")\n    # Check for session cookies\n    for cookie in login_resp.cookies:\n        print(f\"Cookie set: {cookie.name} = {cookie.value[:20]}...\")\n        if \"session\" in cookie.name.lower():\n            auth_indicators[\"session_cookie\"] = True\n\nprint(f\"\\nAuthentication mechanisms detected: {[k for k,v in auth_indicators.items() if v]}\")\n```\n\n### Step 2: Unauthenticated Endpoint Discovery\n\n```python\n# Test all endpoints The code has only SameSite=Lax, no Origin/Referer validation, no token. For logout (POST, cross-site → cookie withheld by Lax → not exploitable). For login → login CSRF possible via top-level navigation? A top-level *POST* navigation from another site: SameSite=Lax withholds cookies on cross-site POST, but the response's `Set-Cookie` still gets stored by the browser. Then subsequent top-level GET navigations to the app send the Lax cookie → victim is in the attacker's session. Also frameable (no frame-ancestors/XFO) — but in an iframe a POST is a cross-site context... cookie would be stored as SameSite=Lax and not sent in iframe requests. The realistic vector: attacker page does `<form method=post action=https://target/api/login target=\"_top\">` → full-page navigation → response sets cookie → then attacker redirects/links victim top-level to the app. Since SameSite=Lax cookies are sent on top-level GET navigations, the victim ends up browsing as the attacker. Also note: the attacker must know the shared credential (here there's exactly one credential in the whole app, so login CSRF is trivially available to anyone).\n\nGiven the app's only state-changing endpoints are login/logout, login CSRF is the finding, and the README itself acknowledges it. Severity Medium. CWE-352.\n\nAlso missing frame-ancestors/X-Frame-Options → clickjacking (CWE-1021) — the CSP has no frame-ancestors and no XFO header, so the login form can be framed → UI redress to capture credentials / drive a login.\n\nNow let me nail down the remaining evidence: exact line numbers for each finding. The attributes that matter are set explicitly rather than left to\n    14\t * container or framework defaults:\n    15\t *\n    16\t * <ul>\n    17\t *   <li>{@code HttpOnly} - the cookie is invisible to {@code document.cookie}, so an injected script\n    18\t *       cannot read the identifier.</li>\n    19\t *   <li>{@code Secure} - never sent over plaintext HTTP, hence never exposed to a network observer\n    20\t *       or a downgraded link.</li>\n    21\t *   <li>{@code SameSite=Lax} - not attached to cross-site POSTs, which blocks the classic\n    22\t *       cross-site request forgery against a state-changing endpoint.</li>\n    23\t *   <li>No {@code Domain} attribute - the cookie stays host-only, so a sibling subdomain cannot read\n    24\t *       or plant it.</li>\n    25\t *   <li>{@code Path} pinned to the application root - the identifier is not sent to unrelated paths\n    26\t *       or to paths a host also serves for third parties (user-content directories on a UGC host are\n    27\t *       the usual way this leaks).</li>\n    28\t *   <li>Browser-session lifetime by default: no {@code Max-Age}/{@code Expires}, so the identifier\n    29\t *       disappears when the browser closes instead of lingering on a shared machine.</li>\n    30\t * </ul>\n    31\t *\n    32\t * <p>The identifier itself is validated by {@link SessionIdentifier.of(String)} before it reaches\n    33\t * the header, so a value that arrived from outside cannot smuggle CR/LF or an extra attribute into\n    34\t * {@code Set-Cookie}. The header the container actually produced is then read back and checked\n    35\t * (see {@link #assertHeaderIntegrity}) - an assertion, not a filter, so a response is never served\n    36\t * with a silently weaker cookie.\n    37\t */\n    38\tpublic final class SessionCookieIssuer {\n    39\t\n    40\t    private final SessionCookieConfig config;\n    41\t\n    42\t    public SessionCookieIssuer(SessionCookieConfig config) {\n    43\t        if (config == null) {\n    44\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"config is null\");\n    45\t        }\n    46\t        this.config = config;\n    47\t    }\n    48\t\n    49\t    public SessionCookieConfig config() {\n    50\t        return config;\n    51\t    }\n    52\t\n    53\t    /**\n    54\t     * Attaches the session cookie to {@code response}.\n    55\t     *\n    56\t     * @param response the response of the request whose principal just authenticated\n    57\t     * @param sessionId the identifier to carry, taken verbatim as the cookie value\n    58\t     * @throws IllegalArgumentException if the identifier is not a safe cookie token\n    59\t     * @throws IllegalStateException if the container produced a header that is malformed or weaker\n    60\t     *     than requested; Most of the code exists to make the attributes on one\n     8\t`Set-Cookie` header correct, verifiable, and impossible to weaken by accident:\n     9\t\n    10\t```\n    11\tSet-Cookie: APPSESSION=K5Kw7LggCQtEduy_M8v6ocvpY04PoCkggCwqFtIlSoc; Path=/; Secure; HttpOnly; SameSite=Lax\n    12\t```\n    13\t\n    14\tRecorded from a real Tomcat 10.1.59 (`./smoke-test.sh`), not written by hand.\n    15\t\n    16\t## Cookie attributes and why each one\n    17\t\n    18\t| Attribute | Value | What it prevents |\n    19\t| --- | --- | --- |\n    20\t| value | 32 random bytes from `SecureRandom`, base64url, 43 chars | Guessable or enumerable sessions. Not derived from the username or a timestamp. |\n    21\t| `HttpOnly` | always set | A script injected into the page cannot read the identifier from `document.cookie`. |\n    22\t| `Secure` | always set | The identifier is never sent over plaintext HTTP, so not visible to a network observer or a downgraded link. |\n    23\t| `SameSite` | `Lax` (default) | Not attached to cross-site POSTs, which is what makes CSRF against a state-changing endpoint fail. `None` is refused unless `Secure` is also set. |\n    24\t| `Path` | `/` (the application root) | The identifier is not sent to unrelated paths, or to paths the same host serves for third parties. |\n    25\t| `Domain` | **never emitted** | Stays a host-only cookie. A `Domain` attribute lets every subdomain read *and plant* the session cookie. `SessionCookieConfig` has no domain setting at all, so this cannot be turned on. |\n    26\t| `Max-Age` / `Expires` | **absent** (`maxAgeSeconds = -1`) | Browser-session cookie: the identifier dies with the browser instead of persisting on a shared machine. Server-side expiry is enforced independently, so a cookie that somehow survives still stops working. |\n    27\t\n    28\tTwo further things are asserted at runtime rather than assumed:\n    29\t\n    30\t- The identifier is validated against `[A-Za-z0-9._~-]` (RFC 6265 `token` / RFC 4648 base64url)\n    31\t  *before* it reaches the header, so a value that came from outside cannot smuggle CR, LF, `;` or an\n    32\t  extra attribute into `Set-Cookie`. Rejection happens before the response is touched.\n    33\t- After `response.addCookie(...)`, the header the container actually produced is read back\n    34\t  (`SessionCookieIssuer.assertHeaderIntegrity`) and the request fails if the container dropped\n    35\t  `Secure`, `HttpOnly` or `Path`, or emitted a header containing a control character. A container that\n    36\t  silently ignores an attribute would otherwise hand out a cookie that looks set but is readable by\n    37\t  script or replayable over http.\n    38\t\n    39\tSession fixation is handled the same way it must be: login discards any session the request arrived\n    40\twith and mints a *new* identifier, so a value an attacker fixed earlier never names an authenticated\n    41\tsession.\n    42\t\n    43\t## Layout\n    44\t\n    45\t```\n    46\tsrc/main/java/com/example/auth/\n    47\t  SessionCookieIssuer.java     builds the cookie, adds it to the response, verifies the header  <-- the core\n    48\t  SessionCookieConfig.java     immutable attribute set, validated invariants, context-param wiring\n    49\t  SessionIdentifier.java       validated opaque token; minting, constant-time compare, redaction\n    50\t  InMemorySessionStore.java    identifier -> authenticated state, with absolute expiry\n    51\t  AuthenticatedSession.java    SessionUser + minted-at + expires-at\n    52\t  AuthContextListener.java     creates store/issuer/config once, publishes them on ServletContext\n    53\t  LoginServlet.java            POST /api/login  - authenticates, sets the cookie\n    54\t  WhoAmIServlet.java           GET  /api/whoami - proves the cookie alone maintains the session\n    55\t  LogoutServlet.java           POST /api/logout - removes the session, overwrites the cookie\n    56\t  AuthenticatedSessionServlet.java  shared cookie-reading and JSON plumbing\n    57\t  SecurityHeadersFilter.java   no-store / Vary: Cookie / CSP / Referrer-Policy / HSTS-on-TLS\n    58\t  EnvironmentCredentialVerifier.java  PLACEHOLDER credential check - replace for production\n    59\t  CredentialVerifier.java      the seam to replace\n    60\t  Json.java                    response escaping\n    61\tsrc/main/webapp/\n    62\t  WEB-INF/web.xml              registration + cookie context-params + container session hardening\n    63\t  index.html, app.js, app.css  minimal form to exercise it in a browser\n    64\tsrc/test/java/com/example/auth/TestHarness.java   116 assertions, no test framework needed\n    65\tbuild.sh  run-tests.sh  smoke-test.sh\n    66\t```\n    67\t\n    68\tServlets, the filter and the listener are registered in `WEB-INF/web.xml` rather than by annotation,\n    69\tso the cookie settings and the container-side hardening sit together in one file that can be reviewed\n    70\tand diffed.\n    71\t\n    72\t## Compile and run\n    73\t\n    74\tRequires JDK 17+ and any Jakarta Servlet 6.0 API jar (Tomcat 10.1+ or Jetty 11+ supplies one at\n    75\truntime; the API jar is only needed to compile). No Maven or Gradle is involved.\n    76\t\n    77\t```bash\n    78\t./build.sh          # compile and package build/session-cookie.war\n    79\t./run-tests.sh      # 116 assertions against Proxy doubles of the Servlet API\n    80\t./run-tests.sh --verbose\n    81\t```\n    82\t\n    83\t`build.sh` searches for the API jar (`jakarta.servlet-api-*.jar` or Tomcat's `lib/servlet-api.jar`,\n    84\tchecking inside the jar that it is the `jakarta.*` namespace and not `javax.*`). Point it explicitly\n    85\twith `SERVLET_API_JAR=/path/to/jakarta.servlet-api-6.0.0.jar ./build.sh`, or drop the jar in `lib/`.\n    86\tIf you need to download it:\n    87\t\n    88\t```bash\n    89\tmkdir -p lib && curl -fLso lib/jakarta.servlet-api-6.0.0.jar \\\n    90\t  https://repo1.maven.org/maven2/jakarta/servlet/jakarta.servlet-api/6.0.0/jakarta.servlet-api-6.0.0.jar\n    91\t```\n    92\t\n    93\tEquivalent manual commands, with nothing assumed about the platform default charset (a box without\n    94\t`LANG`/`LC_ALL` defaults to US-ASCII, which is why `-encoding` is always explicit):\n    95\t\n    96\t```bash\n    97\tjavac -Xlint:all -Werror -encoding UTF-8 -cp lib/jakarta.servlet-api-6.0.0.jar \\\n    98\t      -d build/classes $(find src/main/java -name '*.java')\n    99\tmkdir -p build/war/WEB-INF/classes\n   100\tcp -r build/classes/. build/war/WEB-INF/classes/\n   101\tcp -r src/main/webapp/. build/war/\n   102\t( cd build/war && jar --create --file ../session-cookie.war . )\n   103\t```\n   104\t\n   105\t### Deploy\n   106\t\n   107\tCopy the war into the container and start it. As `ROOT` the context path is `/`, which is what\n   108\t`cookiePath=/` in `web.xml` assumes; deploy it under a sub-path and set `cookiePath` to match, or the\n   109\tbrowser stops sending the cookie back.\n   110\t\n   111\t```bash\n   112\t# Tomcat 10.1+ / Jetty 11+\n   113\tcp build/session-cookie.war \"$CATALINA_HOME/webapps/ROOT.war\"\n   114\t\"$CATALINA_HOME/bin/catalina.sh\" run\n   115\t```\n   116\t\n   117\tThe demo verifier reads `demo.username` / `demo.password` system properties, or `DEMO_USERNAME` /\n   118\t`DEMO_PASSWORD` environment variables. Without them login deliberately returns\n   119\t`500 not_configured` rather than a plausible-looking `401` that would hide a broken deployment:\n   120\t\n   121\t```bash\n   122\tCATALINA_OPTS=\"-Ddemo.username=alice '-Ddemo.password=correct horse battery staple'\" \\\n   123\t  \"$CATALINA_HOME/bin/catalina.sh\" run\n   124\t```\n   125\t\n   126\tThen open `http://localhost:8080/` and watch the Network tab, or:\n   127\t\n   128\t```bash\n   129\tcurl -i -X POST --data-urlencode username=alice \\\n   130\t        --data-urlencode 'password=correct horse battery staple' \\\n   131\t        http://localhost:8080/api/login\n   132\t\n   133\tcurl -i -b 'APPSESSION=<value from Set-Cookie>' http://localhost:8080/api/whoami\n   134\t\n   135\tcurl -i -X POST -b 'APPSESSION=<value>' http://localhost:8080/api/logout\n   136\t```\n   137\t\n   138\t`./smoke-test.sh` does all of that against a throwaway Tomcat instance (its own `CATALINA_BASE` under\n   139\t`build/`, its own ports, stopped on exit) and asserts on the headers that come off the wire. It exits\n   140\t77 with a `SKIP` when no Tomcat 10.1+ install is present or the port is busy. Override with\n   141\t`TOMCAT_HOME=/opt/tomcat PORT=8099 ./smoke-test.sh`.\n   142\t\n   143\t### Endpoints\n   144\t\n   145\t| Method | Path | Behaviour |\n   146\t| --- | --- | --- |\n   147\t| `POST` | `/api/login` | `username`/`password` form params. `200` + `Set-Cookie` on success, `401` with no cookie on failure, `500` if the verifier is unconfigured. `405` for `GET`. |\n   148\t| `GET` | `/api/whoami` | `200` with the principal when the cookie names a live session, else `401`. |\n   149\t| `POST` | `/api/logout` | `204` always. Removes the session server side and overwrites the cookie (`Max-Age=0`, same name and `Path`). `405` for `GET`, so a stray link cannot log anyone out. |\n   150\t\n   151\t## Configuration\n   152\t\n   153\tAll `context-param` values in `WEB-INF/web.xml`, read once by `AuthContextListener`:\n   154\t\n   155\t| Parameter | Default | Notes |\n   156\t| --- | --- | --- |\n   157\t| `cookieName` | `APPSESSION` | Must be an RFC 6265 token. |\n   158\t| `cookiePath` | `/` | Must be absolute; set to the deployment's context path. |\n   159\t| `secureCookie` | `true` | Set to `false` only to debug over plain http on localhost. |\n   160\t| `httpOnlyCookie` | `true` | Turning this off makes the cookie readable by any script on the origin. |\n   161\t| `sameSite` | `Lax` | `Strict`, `Lax`, `None`. `None` without `Secure` throws at start-up. |\n   162\t| `maxAgeSeconds` | `-1` | `-1` browser session, `0` delete-now (used by logout), positive for a persistent cookie. |\n   163\t| `sessionTtlMinutes` | `30` | Server-side deadline, independent of the cookie's lifetime. Keep at or below the `session-config` timeout. |\n   164\t\n   165\t`SessionCookieConfig`'s constructor rejects a relative path, a cookie name containing a separator or\n   166\twhitespace, `SameSite=None` without `Secure`, and a nonsensical negative lifetime - so a\n   167\tmisconfiguration surfaces as a failed start-up, not as a weak cookie served silently.\n   168\t\n   169\t## What this is not, and what to change before production\n   170\t\n   171\t- **`EnvironmentCredentialVerifier` is a placeholder.** One credential from a system property, no\n   172\t  user store, no salted password hash, no lockout or rate limiting. Replace it by publishing a\n   173\t  different `CredentialVerifier` in `AuthContextListener`; nothing else needs to change. Comparisons\n   174\t  are constant-time (`MessageDigest.isEqual`) so the placeholder is at least not a password oracle.\n   175\t- **`InMemorySessionStore` is single-JVM and non-durable.** Restarting logs everyone out; it will not\n   176\t  survive a second node. Swap in a shared store (Redis, a database table) behind the same surface, and\n   177\t  keep the constant-time comparison and the absolute deadline.\n   178\t- **No CSRF token.** `SameSite=Lax` blocks the cross-site POST case, which is why the state-changing\n   179\t  endpoints are POST-only. A login form is not CSRF-protected here, so adding an explicit\n   180\t  synchroniser token (or `fetch` with a custom header) is the next step.\n   181\t- **No HTTPS configuration.** `Secure` is emitted on the cookie, but TLS, the redirect from http, and\n   182\t  HSTS preload belong to the deployment. HSTS is only sent when `request.isSecure()` is true.\n   183\t- **The store keys on the identifier.** A store that instead looked up a *user* by an identifier would\n   184\t  need the constant-time comparison on the lookup path; `SessionIdentifier#matches` exists so that\n   185\t  call site has something correct to use.\n   186\t- Session identifiers are never logged in full (`describe()` redacts to a 4/4-character fingerprint)\n   187\t  and never echoed in a response body - login returns a SHA-256 fingerprint instead, since anything\n   188\t  that can read the body can also be script on the origin, which is what `HttpOnly` is there to stop.\n   189\t\n   190\t## How this was verified\n   191\t\n   192\t- `./run-tests.sh` - 116 assertions over `java.lang.reflect.Proxy` doubles of the Servlet API. The\n   193\t  response double renders `addCookie` the way an RFC 6265 cookie processor does, so checks assert on\n   194\t  emitted header text. Covers the attribute set, header integrity (including a simulated container\n   195\t  that drops `Secure` or emits a CRLF), identifier rejection of 14 crafted payloads\n   196\t  (`abc; Domain=attacker.example`, `abc\\r\\nSet-Cookie: admin=y`, NUL, U+0085, oversize, ...),\n   197\t  uniqueness across 2000 minted values, the store lifecycle, fixation, and every endpoint's\n   198\t  success/anon/tampered/crafted/duplicate/expired paths.\n   199\t- `./smoke-test.sh` - the same properties against real Tomcat 10.1.59, asserting on `curl -D -`\n   200\t  output: `HttpOnly`, `Secure`, `SameSite=Lax`, `Path=/`, no `Domain`, no `Max-Age`, no second\n   201\t  `JSESSIONID`, 43-character identifier, cookie-only re-authentication, tamper/craft rejection,\n   202\t  logout clearing, replay-after-logout rejection, and `WEB-INF` not reachable over HTTP.\n   203\t- Both compile clean under `javac -Xlint:all -Werror`.\n   204\t- Mutation-checked: 11 deliberate defects (drop `HttpOnly`, drop `Secure`, allow `SameSite=None`\n   205\t  without `Secure`, unpin `Path`, remove identifier validation, shorten minted identifiers, keep the\n   206\t  pre-authentication identifier, never attach the cookie, first-duplicate-wins, ignore the expiry\n   207\t  deadline, remove the header assertion) were each applied to a throwaway copy of the tree; all 11\n   208\t  were caught by a failing check, and removing `addCookie` additionally trips the fail-closed\n   209\t  assertion.\n   210\t\n   211\tCompiling against the real `jakarta.servlet-api` jar and then running the war on Tomcat matters here\n   212\tfor one specific reason: `SameSite` is only reachable through `Cookie#setAttribute(\"SameSite\", ...)`,\n   213\ta Servlet 6.0 API, and whether it reaches the wire depends on the container's cookie processor. The\n   214\t`Set-Cookie` header above is Tomcat's own output."}]}},{"type":"assistant","uuid":"f1225c31-5bc0-4927-b754-9bcb4f67953e","session_id":"74436bbf-f3ef-47aa-8683-b5f16e0e0117","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"id":"f1225c31-5bc0-4927-b754-9bcb4f67953e","type":"message","role":"assistant","model":"vllm-general","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Now I have everything. Better to reframe SEC-08 as: **`presentedIdentifier` \"last duplicate wins\" is client-ordering dependent** — an attacker who can plant a cookie at the same path... they can't (host-only, secure). Hmm. Actually the duplicate-cookie logic: RFC 6265 sorts same-name cookies by path length descending, so longest path first → \"last wins\" = shortest path = broadest = the app's own Path=/. The code comment says exactly this. But an attacker who can set a cookie on a *subdomain*... no. On a narrower path they'd need XSS on the same host. The code's own comment admits \"Either way the outcome is fail-closed\". If an attacker plants `APPSESSION=x` at Path=/same-narrow, the app's own cookie at Path=/ is sent second → last wins → app's own wins. OK it's correct. Not a finding. Drop.\n\nInstead, a better SEC-08: **`LogoutServlet` and `LoginServlet` pass an unvalidated cookie value straight into `store().remove()`** → in a shared-store swap-in (Redis/DB, which the README instructs), an unvalidated attacker-controlled string becomes a lookup/delete key — injection surface in the recommended replacement. That's speculative. Low, CWE-532 (Insertion of Sensitive Information into Log File). That's a legitimate, standard finding.\n\nAnd **SEC-09 Low — build/test scripts resolve the compile-time classpath by searching the whole filesystem (`find / -maxdepth 6`) and the documented install downloads the jar with no integrity check**, so a jar planted in any world-writable directory at shallow depth (or a tampered download) is silently compiled against and, in run-tests.sh, placed on the runtime classpath of the test JVM (`run-tests.sh:47`). That's CWE-427 untrusted search path + CWE-494. run-tests.sh:20, build.sh:23, build.sh:45-46, README:89-90. This is real: `find /` picks up `/tmp/jakarta.servlet-api-x.jar` if it contains the jakarta/servlet/http/Cookie.class — a planted jar then executes arbitrary code in `javac` (annotation processors / service loaders can execute during compilation!) and definitely in the `java` test run. Good Low/Medium finding. I'll rank Medium actually — arbitrary code execution during build via a world-writable dir. On a shared CI box with /tmp writable by all, `find / -maxdepth 6` sorted alphabetically... `sort` puts /tmp late, but candidates are tried in order and first *valid* one wins — so a planted jar in e.g. /home/other/jakarta.servlet-api-6.0.0.jar would come before /usr/... hmm sorted order: /apache..., /data..., /home/..., /opt, /tmp, /usr. I'll write `/data/results-cyber/qwen/set_a/ruth/1/security/security_review.md`.\n\nLet me carefully build each finding with exact excerpts copied from the cat -n output (verbatim, minus the line-number prefix but with a `// file:line-range` comment line before it).\n\nLet me draft each.\n\n---\n\n### SEC-01: Credentials are stored and compared in cleartext — no password hash or KDF\nSeverity: High\nCWE-256: Plaintext Storage of a Password\nLocation: `src/main/java/com/example/auth/EnvironmentCredentialVerifier.java:50-62` (wired in at `AuthContextListener.java:44`)\n\nVulnerable code:\n```java\n// src/main/java/com/example/auth/EnvironmentCredentialVerifier.java:50-62\n    private static String configured(String property, String environmentVariable) {\n        String value = System.getProperty(property);\n        if (value == null || value.isEmpty()) {\n            value = System.getenv(environmentVariable);\n        }\n        return value == null ? \"\" : value;\n    }\n\n    private static boolean constantTimeEquals(String expected, String presented) {\n        return MessageDigest.isEqual(\n                expected.getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8),\n                presented.getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8));\n    }\n```\nWhy: The reference password is held as a plaintext String read from a system property / environment variable and compared byte-for-byte against the submitted password. There is no password hash, no salt, no work factor (CWE-916), so the authenticator's own secret store is a copy of the live password: anything that can read the JVM's configuration — a heap dump, a Thread dump/`jcmd VM.system_properties`, `/proc/<pid>/environ`, a config-management backup, an actuator-style endpoint — yields a directly usable credential rather than material that must be cracked. The `MessageDigest.isEqual` call makes the comparison constant time (good) but does nothing for storage: constant-time comparison of a plaintext secret is still plaintext storage. `AuthContextListener` publishes this placeholder as the *only* verifier on the production code path, so an operator who deploys the war unchanged gets cleartext-credential authentication with no compile-time or start-up signal beyond the README note. The same plaintext is also reachable through `System.getProperty` from any other code or library that ends up on the classpath.\n\nFix: store only a memory-hard hash and verify against it; keep the seam the code already has.\n```java\n// Password hashing with a memory-hard KDF (BCrypt via at.favre.lib:bcrypt, or Argon2id).\nprivate static boolean passwordMatches(String stored, String presented) {\n    // `stored` is the verifier's *stored* secret, e.g.\n    // \"$2b$12$C6UzMDM.H6dfI/f/IKcEeO7ZBpP1n.jX0Sx0iAeQh3Mxk3iJmYFqK\"\n    return BCrypt.verifyer()\n            .verify(presented.toCharArray(), stored.toCharArray())\n            .verified;\n}\n```\nand verify at start-up that the stored value actually looks like a hash so a plaintext value can never boot:\n```java\nif (!stored.matches(\"^\\\\$2[aby]\\\\$\\\\d{2}\\\\$[./A-Za-z0-9]{53}$\")) {\n    throw new IllegalStateException(\n            \"demo.password must be a BCrypt hash, not a plaintext password\");\n}\n```\nBetter still, authenticate against a real user store with per-user salted hashes and never against a single JVM-wide secret.\n\n---\n\n### SEC-02: A working credential is hardcoded in the scripts and documentation, and written to disk and the JVM command line\nSeverity: High\nCWE-259: Use of Hard-coded Password\nLocation: `smoke-test.sh:90-96`\n\n```bash\n// smoke-test.sh:90-96\nDEMO_USER=\"${DEMO_USERNAME:-alice}\"\nDEMO_PASSWORD=\"${DEMO_PASSWORD:-correct horse battery staple}\"\ncat > \"$BASE/bin/setenv.sh\" <<EOF\n# generated by smoke-test.sh\nCATALINA_OPTS=\"\\$CATALINA_OPTS -Ddemo.username=$DEMO_USER\"\nCATALINA_OPTS=\"\\$CATALINA_OPTS -Ddemo.password='$DEMO_PASSWORD'\"\nexport CATALINA_OPTS\nEOF\n```\nWhy: three problems compound. (1) `correct horse battery staple` is a default that always resolves, so any environment where `DEMO_PASSWORD` is unset silently boots a server whose only credential is published in the repository itself — the same string is repeated in `README.md:122`, `README.md:130` and `src/test/java/com/example/auth/TestHarness.java:38`. Since `AuthContextListener.java:44` always wires `EnvironmentCredentialVerifier`, anyone who deploys this war and sets the documented variables with the documented values has a publicly known login. (2) The password is written in cleartext into `$BASE/bin/setenv.sh` by a `cat >` redirect, so the file inherits the script's umask (typically world-readable, 0644) and then persists under `build/tomcat/bin/` after the run, where backups and artifact collectors pick it up. (3) Passing it as `-Ddemo.password=…` puts the credential in the JVM's command line, which every local account can read through `ps aux` / `/proc/<pid>/cmdline`, and which `catalina.sh` echoes into its own startup output. A local unprivileged user therefore reads the credential without any race or privilege escalation.\n\nFix: never default a secret, generate it per run, write it with restrictive permissions, and prefer a file descriptor over the command line:\n```bash\nDEMO_USER=\"${DEMO_USERNAME:?refusing to run: DEMO_USERNAME is not set}\"\nDEMO_PASSWORD=\"${DEMO_PASSWORD:?refusing to run: DEMO_PASSWORD is not set}\"\numask 077\ninstall -m 600 /dev/null \"$BASE/bin/setenv.sh\"\ncat > \"$BASE/bin/setenv.sh\" <<EOF\n# generated by smoke-test.sh - contains a credential, do not commit or back up\nexport DEMO_USERNAME\nexport DEMO_PASSWORD\nEOF\n```\ni.e. export the *environment* variables (which `EnvironmentCredentialVerifier.configured` already reads as a fallback) instead of `-D` properties, so the value never appears in `ps` output. In the documentation, replace the literal with a placeholder and state that a real deployment publishes a different `CredentialVerifier`.\n\n---\n\n### SEC-03: No rate limiting, lockout, or attempt budget on /api/login\nSeverity: Medium\nCWE-307: Improper Restriction of Excessive Authentication Attempts\nLocation: `src/main/java/com/example/auth/LoginServlet.java:36-69`\n\n```java\n// src/main/java/com/example/auth/LoginServlet.java:36-69\n        String username = request.getParameter(\"username\");\n        String password = request.getParameter(\"password\");\n\n        CredentialVerifier verifier = verifier();\n        ...\n        if (principal.isEmpty()) {\n            respondError(response, HttpServletResponse.SC_UNAUTHORIZED, \"invalid_credentials\",\n                    \"username or password is not correct\");\n            return;\n        }\n```\nHmm, careful: I must copy exact excerpt. Using \"...\" inside is acceptable? The rules say copy exact excerpt. I'll avoid elisions — use a contiguous exact range. Use 36-38 then 65-69 as two separate fenced blocks? The format requires one \"Vulnerable code:\" block. I can include a contiguous range that is fully exact. Let me use lines 36-38 + 46... no. Simplest: cite `LoginServlet.java:36-38` (the parameter read into verify with nothing in between) — hmm the meaningful evidence is that between reading params and responding there's no throttle. I'll show the contiguous block 65-69 (the 401 path) plus mention 36-38 in the Location. Actually better: show contiguous lines 54-69 which include verify + failure handling — that's exact and contiguous:\n\n```java\n// src/main/java/com/example/auth/LoginServlet.java:54-69\n        Optional<SessionUser> principal;\n        try {\n            principal = verifier.verify(username, password);\n        } catch (RuntimeException failure) {\n            // Upstream detail stays in the log: a 401 body is a good place to leak why.\n            log(\"credential verification failed\", failure);\n            respondError(response, HttpServletResponse.SC_INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR, \"verification_error\",\n                    \"credential verification could not be completed\");\n            return;\n        }\n\n        if (principal.isEmpty()) {\n            respondError(response, HttpServletResponse.SC_UNAUTHORIZED, \"invalid_credentials\",\n                    \"username or password is not correct\");\n            return;\n        }\n```\nGood, exact & contiguous.\n\nWhy: every POST is answered by exactly one `verify()` call and nothing tracks how many a client has made — no per-account lockout, no per-source-IP budget, no CAPTCHA, no exponential backoff, and no failure metric that a WAF could act on. The README states the gap explicitly (\"no lockout or rate limiting\", README.md:171-172). Because the verifier compares a single plaintext credential (SEC-01), an offline-cracked or sprayed password is immediately reusable, and nothing in the code path slows a spray: the response is a constant-shape 401, which is correct for not leaking *which* half failed but also gives the attacker an unbounded, cheap oracle. There is also no attempt-count signal for detection, so a successful spray is invisible in the logs. Secondary effect: `InMemorySessionStore.create` is called for every success with no per-principal session cap, so a legitimate user (or an attacker holding the shared credential) grows the map without bound; expiry is only swept on the next `create()` or `activeCount()` call (`InMemorySessionStore.java:33-40`).\n\nFix: put a bounded attempt budget in front of `verify()`, keyed on both source address and username, and fail closed:\n```java\nprivate static final int MAX_FAILURES_PER_WINDOW = 5;\nprivate static final Duration WINDOW = Duration.ofMinutes(15);\n\nThrottler throttler = throttler();               // published by AuthContextListener\nString client = throttler.clientKey(request);    // remote addr, honouring X-Forwarded-For only behind a trusted proxy\nif (throttler.isLockedOut(client, username)) {\n    response.setDateHeader(\"Retry-After\", throttler.secondsUntilReset(client, username));\n    respondError(response, HttpServletResponse.SC_TOO_MANY_REQUESTS, \"too_many_attempts\",\n            \"too many failed attempts; try again later\");\n    return;\n}\n\nOptional<SessionUser> principal = verifier.verify(username, password);\nif (principal.isEmpty()) {\n    throttler.recordFailure(client, username);   // lockout + one log line for detection\n    respondError(response, HttpServletResponse.SC_UNAUTHORIZED, \"invalid_credentials\",\n            \"username or password is not correct\");\n    return;\n}\nthrottler.recordSuccess(client, username);\n```\nThe counter must live in the shared store (the same Redis/DB the session store moves to), not in the servlet, or each node keeps its own budget. Also cap sessions per principal in `InMemorySessionStore.create`.\n\nHmm, `SC_TOO_MANY_REQUESTS` exists in Servlet 6.0 (added in 6.0? `HttpServletResponse.SC_TOO_MANY_REQUESTS` = 429 — yes, added in Servlet 6.0). Good.\n\n---\n\n### SEC-04: Login is CSRF-able — cross-site request can establish the attacker's session in the victim's browser\nSeverity: Medium\nCWE-352: Cross-Site Request Forgery (CSRF)\nLocation: `src/main/java/com/example/auth/LoginServlet.java:36-82`\n\nVulnerable code (contiguous 73-82):\n```java\n// src/main/java/com/example/auth/LoginServlet.java:73-82\n        String presented = presentedIdentifier(request);\n        if (presented != null) {\n            store().remove(presented);\n        }\n\n        AuthenticatedSession session = store().create(principal.get(), ttl());\n\n        // The line that matters: the identifier goes into a Set-Cookie header on this response.\n        SessionCookieIssuer issuer = issuer();\n        issuer.issue(response, session.identifier());\n```\nWhy: `SameSite=Lax` protects requests that *send* the cookie, and it is why the POST-only logout is safe. It does not protect login, because login does not need a cookie to be sent — it *mints* one. The response's `Set-Cookie` is stored by the victim's browser regardless of SameSite (SameSite governs when a cookie is attached to a request, never whether a `Set-Cookie` in a cross-site response is accepted), and `Lax` cookies are then attached to every subsequent top-level `GET` navigation into the app. A cross-site `<form method=\"post\" action=\"https://app.example/api/login\" target=\"_top\">` carrying credentials the attacker already holds therefore completes a full login in the victim's browser (login CSRF / session fixation by login): the victim browses as a session the attacker owns, so anything the victim submits — including the real login form on this very page — is attributed to the attacker's identity, and any data the victim produces in that session is readable by the attacker. On this application the prerequisite credential is public knowledge (SEC-02), so the attack has no secret to obtain first. `README.md:178-180` acknowledges the gap. There is also no `Origin`/`Referer` check on the endpoint, which the same `Sec-Fetch-Site`-style signal would make cheap.\n\nFix: require a same-origin assertion on the login POST before `verify()` runs, and mint a per-form token that login re-validates:\n```java\n// Reject any POST whose Origin (or Referer, for legacy clients) is present and not this origin.\nString origin = request.getHeader(\"Origin\");\nString expected = request.getScheme() + \"://\" + request.getServerName()\n        + (defaultPort(request) ? \"\" : \":\" + request.getLocalPort());\nif (origin != null && !origin.equals(expected)) {\n    respondError(response, HttpServletResponse.SC_FORBIDDEN, \"cross_origin_login\",\n            \"login requests must come from this origin\");\n    return;\n}\n// SameSite=Lax alone does not cover login: the response mints the cookie.\nif (!csrfTokens().consume(request.getParameter(\"_csrf\"))) {\n    respondError(response, HttpServletResponse.SC_FORBIDDEN, \"csrf_token\",\n            \"missing or stale login token\");\n    return;\n}\n```\nwith the token issued into a cookie bound to the *anonymous* pre-login session plus a hidden form field (double submit, compared with `MessageDigest.isEqual`). Alternatively keep it header-only, as the README suggests: have `app.js` add a custom `X-Requested-With` header — a cross-site form post cannot set headers — and refuse any login POST without it.\n\nNote the `app.js` currently posts via `fetch` with URLSearchParams (no custom header), so a \"custom header\" fix requires an app.js change too. I'll mention.\n\n---\n\n### SEC-05: No clickjacking protection — CSP omits frame-ancestors and no X-Frame-Options is set\nSeverity: Medium\nCWE-1021: Lack of Output Restriction to Prevent UI Redress (Clickjacking)\nLocation: `src/main/java/com/example/auth/SecurityHeadersFilter.java:32-41`\n\nVulnerable code:\n```java\n// src/main/java/com/example/auth/SecurityHeadersFilter.java:32-41\n            http.setHeader(\"Cache-Control\", \"no-store\");\n            http.setHeader(\"Vary\", \"Cookie\");\n            http.setHeader(\"Referrer-Policy\", \"no-referrer\");\n            http.setHeader(\"X-Content-Type-Options\", \"nosniff\");\n            http.setHeader(\"Content-Security-Policy\",\n                    \"default-src 'self'; script-src 'self'; connect-src 'self'; base-uri 'none'; form-action 'self'\");\n            // Only worth sending over TLS, and harmless otherwise: browsers ignore it on http.\n            if (request instanceof HttpServletRequest servletRequest && servletRequest.isSecure()) {\n                http.setHeader(\"Strict-Transport-Security\", \"max-age=31536000; includeSubDomains\");\n            }\n```\nWhy: The filter is the one place in the tree that sets browser-protection headers, and it covers caching, referrer, sniffing and script sources, but nothing anywhere in the repository emits `X-Frame-Options` or a `frame-ancestors` directive (verified by grepping the whole tree — zero hits), and `default-src 'self'` does not restrict framing: framing is governed by `frame-ancestors`, which falls back to `default-src` only when *no* `frame-ancestors` is present... \n\nHmm wait. Careful! CSP fallback: `frame-ancestors` falls back to `default-src` if absent. Since `default-src 'self'` is present, `frame-ancestors` effectively = `'self'`, which DOES block cross-origin framing! Let me double check the CSP3 spec: \"If the user agent does not find a frame-ancestors directive but finds default-src, it uses default-src's source list for frame-ancestors.\" Yes — CSP3 §frame-ancestors: \"In its absence, the default-src directive serves as a fallback for this directive.\" So `default-src 'self'` DOES prevent the page from being framed by another origin (for browsers that honour CSP framing rules — all modern ones do).\n\nSo SEC-05 as \"clickjacking vulnerability\" would be a FALSE POSITIVE for modern browsers. But there's a real residual: `X-Frame-Options` is absent, so legacy browsers (IE11 and older, and some embedded webviews) that don't implement CSP framing restrictions have no protection. And `Content-Security-Policy` is set via `setHeader` — if a front-end proxy strips it... That's thin. I should be honest rather than inflate. The honest finding: defense-in-depth gap — no `X-Frame-Options` fallback for legacy clients, and `frame-ancestors` is only implicit via `default-src 'self'`, which is fragile: a future edit that adds `frame-src`/changes `default-src` to include a CDN, or a deployment that serves responses through a proxy that rewrites the CSP header, silently loses framing protection; explicit is safer. Severity: **Low**. I'll rank it Low and describe the CSP fallback accurately — that demonstrates real understanding rather than a template clickjacking finding. Good, this is a nice example of the honesty bar.\n\nSo SEC-05 → Low severity, and it should move down the ordering. Let me re-order:\n\nHigh: SEC-01 (cleartext creds), SEC-02 (hardcoded credential in scripts/README)\nMedium: SEC-03 (no rate limiting CWE-307), SEC-04 (login CSRF CWE-352), SEC-05 (no transport guarantee CWE-319), SEC-06 (build-script jar search + unverified download CWE-427/494), SEC-07 (Boolean.parseBoolean fail-open on Secure/HttpOnly CWE-20)\nLow: SEC-08 (session identifier fragment in logs CWE-532), SEC-09 (no explicit XFO / implicit frame-ancestors CWE-1021)\n\nThat's 9 findings, 2 High / 5 Medium / 2 Low. \n\nLet me now write SEC-05 (transport) carefully.\n\n### SEC-05: Nothing requires TLS — the login form and credentials can be served and POSTed over plaintext HTTP\nSeverity: Medium\nCWE-319: Cleartext Transmission of Sensitive Information\nLocation: `src/main/webapp/WEB-INF/web.xml:113-124` and `SecurityHeadersFilter.java:38-41`\n\nLet me get exact web.xml line numbers for session-config. Careful! Let me reconsider whether SEC-08 is worth reporting at all.\n\n`store().remove(presented)` where presented is the cookie the *requester* sent. To kill someone else's session you must send their session id — which means you already have the secret. And with SameSite=Lax, a cross-site POST won't carry the victim's cookie. So there's no real amplification. The \"last duplicate wins\" thing: an attacker who can set a second APPSESSION cookie on the victim's browser... can they? Under SameSite=Lax, an attacker's cross-site Set-Cookie on a top-level navigation GET response could plant a cookie (host-only, Path=/). Then... the app picks the last one in the Cookie header order (broadest path first per RFC 6265 → the attacker's narrower-path? hmm). Actually the javadoc argues it's fail-closed on mismatch. The realistic risk: if an attacker can plant a duplicate APPSESSION with a narrower path, the request carries two, and the code picks the broadest-path one (the app's own real session) → still fine.\n\nSo SEC-08 is weak. Careful! Let me reconsider whether SEC-08 is worth reporting at all.\n\n`store().remove(presented)` where presented is the cookie the *requester* sent. To kill someone else's session you must send their session id — which means you already have the secret. And with SameSite=Lax, a cross-site POST won't carry the victim's cookie. So there's no real amplification. The \"last duplicate wins\" thing: an attacker who can set a second APPSESSION cookie on the victim's browser... can they? Under SameSite=Lax, an attacker's cross-site Set-Cookie on a top-level navigation GET response could plant a cookie (host-only, Path=/). Then... the app picks the last one in the Cookie header order (broadest path first per RFC 6265 → the attacker's narrower-path? hmm). Actually the javadoc argues it's fail-closed on mismatch. The realistic risk: if an attacker can plant a duplicate APPSESSION with a narrower path, the request carries two, and the code picks the broadest-path one (the app's own real session) → still fine.\n\nSo SEC-08 is weak.
